    85	expect() { local name=$1 pattern=$2 file=$3; if grep -Eq -- "$pattern" "$file" 2>/dev/null; then result "$name" ok; else result "$name" no; echo "      expected /$pattern/ in $file"; fi; }
    86	refuse() { local name=$1 pattern=$2 file=$3; if [ ! -f "$file" ]; then result "$name" no; echo "      missing $file"; elif grep -Eq -- "$pattern" "$file"; then result "$name" no; echo "      unexpected /$pattern/ in $file"; else result "$name" ok; fi; }
    87	# check NAME COMMAND...: PASS when COMMAND exits 0. Its output explains a FAIL.
    88	check() {
    89	  local name=$1 out
    90	  shift
    91	  if out=$("$@" 2>&1); then result "$name" ok; else result "$name" no; printf '%s\n' "$out" | sed 's/^/      /'; fi
    92	}
    93	# info NAME COMMAND...: an INFO line for platform behavior that is recorded,

    95	info() {
    96	  local name=$1 out answer=no
    97	  shift
    98	  if out=$("$@" 2>&1); then answer=yes; fi

   100	  if [ -n "$out" ]; then printf '%s\n' "$out" | sed 's/^/      /'; fi

   104	# Plugin list helpers. FILE holds "id version [path]" lines from inspect plugins.
   105	has_plugin() {
   106	  local file=$1 id=$2 version=${3:-}
   107	  if awk -v id="$id" -v v="$version" '$1 == id && (v == "" || $2 == v) { found = 1 } END { exit !found }' "$file"; then return 0; fi
   108	  echo "expected $id${version:+ $version}; installed: $(awk '{ printf "%s %s; ", $1, $2 }' "$file")"
   109	  return 1
   110	}
   111	lacks_plugin() {
   112	  local file=$1 id=$2
   113	  if awk -v id="$id" '$1 == id { found = 1 } END { exit found }' "$file"; then return 0; fi
   114	  echo "unexpected $id; installed: $(awk '{ printf "%s %s; ", $1, $2 }' "$file")"
   115	  return 1
   116	}

   131	  [ "$got" = "$want" ] && return 0
   132	  echo "$dir holds: ${got:-nothing}; expected: $want"

   196	  got=$(claude_session "$log")
   197	  [ "$got" = "$sid" ] || { echo "resumed session is ${got:-missing}, expected $sid"; return 1; }
   198	  grep -Fq "$prompt" "$transcript" || { echo "transcript $transcript lacks the resumed prompt"; return 1; }
   199	}
   200	same_codex_session() {

   202	  got=$(codex_session "$log")
   203	  [ "$got" = "$sid" ] || { echo "resumed session is ${got:-missing}, expected $sid"; return 1; }
   204	  grep -Fq "$prompt" "$rollout" || { echo "rollout $rollout lacks the resumed prompt"; return 1; }

   222	    echo "marketplace: $E2E_REPO at $ref"
   223	  else
   224	    mkt=$work/mkt
   225	    mkdir -p "$mkt"
   226	    copy_tree "$repo" "$mkt"
   227	    git -c init.defaultBranch=main init -q "$mkt"
   228	    commit_all "$mkt" e2e
   229	    claude_source=$mkt
   230	    codex_source=("$mkt")
   231	    echo "marketplace copy: $mkt"
   232	  fi
   233	}
   234	want_version() { [ -n "$mkt" ] && json_field version <"$mkt/plugins/$1/.claude-plugin/plugin.json"; }
   235	# The last line of the skill, which is the last line of the final check.
   236	want_final_line() {
   237	  local file
   238	  if [ -n "$mkt" ]; then file=$mkt/src/adhd-unslop/overlay/90-final-check.md; else file=$1/skills/adhd-unslop/SKILL.md; fi
   239	  awk 'NF { last = $0 } END { print last }' "$file" 2>/dev/null
   240	}
   241	want_bundle() {
   242	  local root=$1
   243	  if [ -n "$mkt" ]; then root=$mkt/plugins/adhd-unslop; fi
   244	  bundle_from_manifest <"$root/hooks/chunks/manifest.json" 2>/dev/null
   245	}

   247	run_claude() {
   248	  setup_source
   249	  new_claude_home claude
   250	  local log=$work/claude-install.log sid transcript bundle root final
   251	  cl plugin marketplace add "$claude_source" >"$log" 2>&1
   252	  cl plugin install adhd-unslop@adhd-unslop >>"$log" 2>&1
   253	  claude_plugins "$work/claude-plugins-1.txt"
   254	  check "claude: installing adhd-unslop installs only adhd-unslop" only_plugins "$work/claude-plugins-1.txt" adhd-unslop@adhd-unslop
   255	  check "claude: adhd-unslop has the expected version" has_plugin "$work/claude-plugins-1.txt" adhd-unslop@adhd-unslop "$(want_version adhd-unslop)"
   256	  refuse "claude: install reports no dependencies" "dependenc" "$log"
   257	  root=$(awk '$1 == "adhd-unslop@adhd-unslop" { print $3 }' "$work/claude-plugins-1.txt")
   258	  bundle=$(want_bundle "$root")
   259	  final=$(want_final_line "$root")
   260	  echo "      bundle id: ${bundle:-unknown}; final check line: ${final:-unknown}"
   261	
   262	  # Always-on is off: no flag file and ADHD_UNSLOP_ALWAYS unset.
   263	  log=$work/claude-invoke.log
   264	  clp "$log" '/adhd-unslop:adhd-unslop Reply with the single word READY.'
   265	  sid=$(claude_session "$log")
   266	  transcript=$(claude_transcript "$sid")
   267	  [ -n "$transcript" ] && cp "$transcript" "$work/claude-invoke.transcript.jsonl"
   268	  expect "claude: /adhd-unslop:adhd-unslop answers" '"result":"READY' "$log"
   269	  check "claude: always-on off injects no chunk" inspect bundle claude "$transcript" "$bundle" none
   270	  check "claude: skill text arrives with both upstream texts and the final check line" \
   271	    inspect skill-arrived claude "$transcript" "$root/skills/adhd-unslop/SKILL.md" "$final"
   272	  check "claude: skill body arrives whole" \
   273	    inspect skill-arrived claude "$transcript" "$root/skills/adhd-unslop/SKILL.md" "$final" whole
   274	
   275	  # The clean case: only adhd-unslop is installed.
   276	  log=$work/claude-unprompted.log
   277	  clp "$log" 'What is 17 times 3? Digits only.'
   278	  expect "claude: unprompted question answers" '"result":"51' "$log"
   279	  check "claude: unprompted question loads no skill" inspect no-skill "$log"
   280	
   281	  # Platform behavior, recorded as INFO: what an invoked skill keeps after
   282	  # compaction. The skill is larger than the 5,000-token re-attach budget.
   283	  log=$work/claude-compact-same.log
   284	  clturns "$log" '/adhd-unslop:adhd-unslop Reply with the single word READY.' '/compact'
   285	  transcript=$(claude_transcript "$(claude_session "$log")")
   286	  [ -n "$transcript" ] && cp "$transcript" "$work/claude-compact-same.transcript.jsonl"
   287	  info "claude: after /compact in the same process, the skill keeps its final check line" \
   288	    inspect compacted "$transcript" adhd-unslop:adhd-unslop "$final"
   289	  log=$work/claude-compact-resume.log
   290	  clp "$log" --resume "$sid" '/compact'
   291	  transcript=$(claude_transcript "$sid")
   292	  [ -n "$transcript" ] && cp "$transcript" "$work/claude-compact-resume.transcript.jsonl"
   293	  info "claude: after --resume then /compact, the skill keeps its final check line" \
   294	    inspect compacted "$transcript" adhd-unslop:adhd-unslop "$final"
   295	
   296	  log=$work/claude-install.log
   297	  cl plugin install au-i-have-adhd@adhd-unslop >>"$log" 2>&1
   298	  cl plugin install au-unslop@adhd-unslop >>"$log" 2>&1
   299	  claude_plugins "$work/claude-plugins-2.txt"
   300	  check "claude: au-i-have-adhd installs by name" has_plugin "$work/claude-plugins-2.txt" au-i-have-adhd@adhd-unslop "$(want_version au-i-have-adhd)"
   301	  check "claude: au-unslop installs by name" has_plugin "$work/claude-plugins-2.txt" au-unslop@adhd-unslop "$(want_version au-unslop)"
   302	
   303	  log=$work/claude-typed-adhd.log
   304	  clp "$log" '/au-i-have-adhd:i-have-adhd Reply with the number of the rule that caps list length, digits only.'
   305	  expect "claude: typed /au-i-have-adhd:i-have-adhd works" '"result":"9[^0-9]' "$log"
   306	
   307	  log=$work/claude-typed-unslop.log
   308	  clp "$log" '/au-unslop:unslop Reply with the number of the rule about em dashes, digits only.'
   309	  expect "claude: typed /au-unslop:unslop works" '"result":"13[^0-9]' "$log"
   310	
   311	  touch "$claude_home/.adhd-unslop-always"
   312	  log=$work/claude-always-on.log
   313	  clp "$log" 'Reply with the single word OK.'
   314	  sid=$(claude_session "$log")
   315	  transcript=$(claude_transcript "$sid")
   316	  [ -n "$transcript" ] && cp "$transcript" "$work/claude-always-on.transcript.jsonl"
   317	  check "claude: always-on delivers one complete bundle at startup" inspect bundle claude "$transcript" "$bundle" exact
   318	
   319	  log=$work/claude-resume.log
   320	  clp "$log" --resume "$sid" 'Reply with the single word AGAIN.'
   321	  check "claude: --resume continues the same session" same_claude_session "$log" "$sid" "$transcript" 'Reply with the single word AGAIN.'
   322	  expect "claude: resume fires SessionStart with source resume" '"hook_name":"SessionStart:resume"' "$log"
   323	  [ -n "$transcript" ] && cp "$transcript" "$work/claude-resume.transcript.jsonl"
   324	  check "claude: resumed transcript holds exactly one copy of each chunk" inspect bundle claude "$transcript" "$bundle" exact
   325	}
   326	
   327	run_codex() {
   328	  setup_source
   329	  new_codex_home codex
   330	  local log=$work/codex-install.log sid rollout bundle version root final
     1	// Parsers for tests/e2e/run.sh. Each subcommand reads one log or transcript
     2	// and prints what it found. Assertion subcommands exit 1 on failure.
     3	//
     4	//   bundle <claude|codex> <file> <bundle-id> <exact|atleast|none>
     5	//     Counts each chunk's END line in the context records only: SessionStart
     6	//     attachments in a Claude Code transcript (attachment.content), developer
     7	//     messages in a Codex rollout. A Claude record also repeats the text in
     8	//     stdout and rendered, so a plain grep would count every copy three times.
     9	//     exact: each chunk once with <bundle-id>, and no END line from another
    10	//     bundle. atleast: each chunk at least once with <bundle-id>. none: no END
    11	//     line at all.
    12	//   skill-arrived <claude|codex> <file> <SKILL.md> <final-line> [whole]
    13	//     One context record holds the skill text: the Claude Code skill message
    14	//     or tool result in a transcript, the injected skill message in a Codex
    15	//     rollout. It must carry both "<!-- BEGIN upstream" lines and <final-line>.
    16	//     With "whole", it must also hold the SKILL.md body, everything after the
    17	//     frontmatter, byte for byte.
    18	//   compacted <transcript> <skill-name> <final-line>
    19	//     After the last compact boundary of a Claude Code transcript, reports the
    20	//     invoked_skills attachment for <skill-name>: its size, whether it was cut,
    21	//     and whether it still holds <final-line>. Exits 0 only when it does.
    22	//   no-skill <stream.jsonl>
    23	//     No Skill tool call and no Read of a SKILL.md or references/ file.
    24	//   session <stream.jsonl>
    25	//     Prints the Claude Code session id.
    26	//   plugins <claude|codex> <list.json>
    27	//     Prints "id version" for each installed plugin, from `plugin list --json`.
    28	//     Claude Code lines add the install path.
    29	//   hooks-trusted <hooks.jsonl> <plugin-id> <version> <key>...
    30	//     Every hook of <plugin-id> in `codex-hooks.mjs list` output is trusted,
    31	//     comes from the <version> cache folder, and the keys are exactly <key>...
    32	
    33	import fs from "node:fs";
    34	
    35	const END = /END adhd-unslop chunk (\d) of 3 \(([0-9a-f]+)\)/g;
    36	
    37	function jsonLines(file) {
    38	  const out = [];
    39	  for (const line of fs.readFileSync(file, "utf8").split("\n")) {
    40	    if (!line.startsWith("{")) continue;
    41	    try {
    42	      out.push(JSON.parse(line));
    43	    } catch {
    44	      // Skip non-JSON lines such as CLI warnings.
    45	    }
    46	  }
    47	  return out;
    48	}
    49	
    50	function texts(value) {
    51	  if (typeof value === "string") return [value];
    52	  if (Array.isArray(value)) return value.flatMap(texts);
    53	  if (value && typeof value === "object") {
    54	    if (typeof value.text === "string") return [value.text];
    55	    if ("content" in value) return texts(value.content);
    56	  }
    57	  return [];
    58	}
    59	
    60	function contextTexts(kind, file) {
    61	  const records = jsonLines(file);
    62	  if (kind === "claude") {
    63	    return records
    64	      .filter((r) => r.type === "attachment" && r.attachment?.hookEvent === "SessionStart")
    65	      .map((r) => texts(r.attachment.content).join("\n"));
    66	  }
    67	  if (kind === "codex") {
    68	    return records
    69	      .filter((r) => r.type === "response_item" && r.payload?.type === "message" && r.payload?.role === "developer")
    70	      .map((r) => texts(r.payload.content).join("\n"));
    71	  }
    72	  throw new Error(`unknown runtime ${kind}`);
    73	}
    74	
    75	function bundle(kind, file, id, mode) {
    76	  const counts = { 1: 0, 2: 0, 3: 0 };
    77	  const others = [];
    78	  for (const text of contextTexts(kind, file)) {
    79	    for (const m of text.matchAll(END)) {
    80	      if (m[2] === id && counts[m[1]] !== undefined) counts[m[1]]++;
    81	      else others.push(`chunk ${m[1]} (${m[2]})`);
    82	    }
    83	  }
    84	  const summary = `chunk 1: ${counts[1]}, chunk 2: ${counts[2]}, chunk 3: ${counts[3]} with ${id}; other END lines: ${others.length ? others.join(", ") : "none"}`;
    85	  const all = [1, 2, 3].map((i) => counts[i]);
    86	  let ok;
    87	  if (mode === "exact") ok = all.every((n) => n === 1) && others.length === 0;
    88	  else if (mode === "atleast") ok = all.every((n) => n >= 1);
    89	  else if (mode === "none") ok = all.every((n) => n === 0) && others.length === 0;
    90	  else throw new Error(`unknown mode ${mode}`);
    91	  console.log(summary);
    92	  return ok;
    93	}
    94	
    95	function toolUses(records) {
    96	  const uses = [];
    97	  for (const r of records) {
    98	    if (r.type !== "assistant") continue;
    99	    for (const c of r.message?.content ?? []) if (c.type === "tool_use") uses.push(c);
   100	  }
   101	  return uses;
   102	}
   103	
   104	const BEGIN_MARKERS = ["<!-- BEGIN upstream i-have-adhd", "<!-- BEGIN upstream unslop"];
   105	
   106	function skillRecords(kind, file) {
   107	  const records = jsonLines(file);
   108	  if (kind === "claude") {
   109	    const out = [];
   110	    for (const r of records) {
   111	      if (r.type === "user" && r.message) out.push({ where: r.isMeta ? "skill message" : "user message", text: texts(r.message.content).join("\n") });
   112	      if (r.type === "attachment" && r.attachment && r.attachment.type !== "prompt_snapshot")
   113	        out.push({ where: `${r.attachment.type} attachment`, text: texts(r.attachment.content ?? r.attachment.skills).join("\n") });
   114	    }
   115	    return out;
   116	  }
   117	  if (kind === "codex") {
   118	    return records
   119	      .filter((r) => r.type === "response_item" && r.payload?.type === "message")
   120	      .map((r) => ({ where: `${r.payload.role} message`, text: texts(r.payload.content).join("\n") }));
   121	  }
   122	  throw new Error(`unknown runtime ${kind}`);
   123	}
   124	
   125	function skillArrived(kind, file, skillPath, finalLine, whole) {
   126	  const markers = [...BEGIN_MARKERS, finalLine];
   127	  const records = skillRecords(kind, file);
   128	  const hit = records.find((r) => markers.every((m) => r.text.includes(m)));
   129	  if (!hit) {
   130	    const best = records
   131	      .map((r) => ({ r, n: markers.filter((m) => r.text.includes(m)).length }))
   132	      .sort((a, b) => b.n - a.n)[0];
   133	    const found = best ? markers.filter((m) => best.r.text.includes(m)) : [];
   134	    console.log(`no record holds all three markers; best: ${best ? `${best.r.where}, ${best.r.text.length} chars, with ${found.join(" | ") || "none"}` : "no records"}`);
   135	    return false;
   136	  }
   137	  if (whole !== "whole") {
   138	    console.log(`${hit.where}, ${hit.text.length} chars`);
   139	    return true;
   140	  }
   141	  const body = fs.readFileSync(skillPath, "utf8").replace(/^---\r?\n[\s\S]*?\r?\n---\r?\n/, "");
   142	  if (hit.text.includes(body)) {
   143	    console.log(`${hit.where} holds all ${body.length} characters of the SKILL.md body`);
   144	    return true;
   145	  }
   146	  let n = 0;
   147	  while (n < body.length && hit.text.includes(body.slice(0, n + 1))) n = Math.min(body.length, n + 256);
   148	  console.log(`${hit.where} (${hit.text.length} chars) lacks part of the ${body.length}-character SKILL.md body; the first ${Math.max(0, n - 256)} characters match`);
   149	  return false;
   150	}
   151	
   152	function compacted(file, skillName, finalLine) {
   153	  const records = jsonLines(file);
   154	  let boundary = -1;
   155	  records.forEach((r, i) => {
   156	    if (r.type === "system" && r.subtype === "compact_boundary") boundary = i;
   157	  });
   158	  if (boundary < 0) {
   159	    console.log("no compact boundary in the transcript");
   160	    return false;
   161	  }
   162	  const meta = records[boundary].compactMetadata ?? {};
   163	  const after = records.slice(boundary + 1);
   164	  const skills = after
   165	    .filter((r) => r.type === "attachment" && r.attachment?.type === "invoked_skills")
   166	    .flatMap((r) => r.attachment.skills ?? [])
   167	    .filter((s) => s.name === skillName);
   168	  const tokens = `${meta.preTokens ?? "?"} to ${meta.postTokens ?? "?"} tokens`;
   169	  if (!skills.length) {
   170	    console.log(`${meta.trigger ?? "?"} compaction, ${tokens}; no ${skillName} in an invoked_skills attachment after it`);
   171	    return false;
   172	  }
   173	  const text = texts(skills[skills.length - 1].content).join("\n");
   174	  const cut = text.includes("truncated for compaction");
   175	  const has = text.includes(finalLine);
   176	  const kept = BEGIN_MARKERS.filter((m) => text.includes(m)).map((m) => m.replace("<!-- BEGIN upstream ", ""));
   177	  console.log(
   178	    `${meta.trigger ?? "?"} compaction, ${tokens}; ${skillName} re-attached with ${text.length} chars${cut ? ", cut for compaction" : ""}; begins kept: ${kept.join(", ") || "none"}; final check line ${has ? "present" : "missing"}`,
   179	  );
   180	  return has;
   181	}
   182	
   183	function noSkill(file) {
   184	  const bad = toolUses(jsonLines(file)).filter(
   185	    (u) => u.name === "Skill" || (u.name === "Read" && /(SKILL\.md|\/references\/)/.test(String(u.input?.file_path ?? ""))),
   186	  );
   187	  for (const u of bad) console.log(`${u.name} ${JSON.stringify(u.input)}`);
   188	  if (!bad.length) console.log("no Skill call and no skill file read");
   189	  return bad.length === 0;
   190	}
   191	
   192	function session(file) {
   193	  const init = jsonLines(file).find((r) => r.type === "system" && r.subtype === "init");
   194	  if (!init?.session_id) return false;
   195	  console.log(init.session_id);
   196	  return true;
   197	}
   198	
   199	function plugins(kind, file) {
   200	  const data = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(file, "utf8"));
   201	  const list = kind === "claude" ? data : data.installed ?? [];
   202	  for (const p of list) {
   203	    if (kind === "claude") console.log(`${p.id} ${p.version} ${p.installPath ?? ""}`.trim());
   204	    else console.log(`${p.pluginId} ${p.version}`);
   205	  }
   206	  return true;
   207	}
   208	
   209	function hooksTrusted(file, pluginId, version, keys) {
   210	  const hooks = jsonLines(file).filter((h) => h.pluginId === pluginId);
   211	  let ok = true;
   212	  for (const h of hooks) {
   213	    const inVersion = String(h.sourcePath ?? "").includes(`/${version}/hooks/`);
   214	    console.log(`${h.key} ${h.trustStatus}${inVersion ? "" : ` from ${h.sourcePath}`}`);
   215	    if (h.trustStatus !== "trusted" || !inVersion) ok = false;
   216	  }
   217	  const got = hooks.map((h) => h.key).sort().join(" ");
   218	  if (got !== [...keys].sort().join(" ")) {
   219	    console.log(`expected keys ${keys.join(" ")}`);
   220	    ok = false;
   221	  }
   222	  return ok;
   223	}
   224	
   225	const [cmd, ...args] = process.argv.slice(2);
   226	const commands = {
   227	  bundle: () => bundle(args[0], args[1], args[2], args[3]),
   228	  "skill-arrived": () => skillArrived(args[0], args[1], args[2], args[3], args[4]),
   229	  compacted: () => compacted(args[0], args[1], args[2]),
   230	  "no-skill": () => noSkill(args[0]),
   231	  session: () => session(args[0]),
   232	  plugins: () => plugins(args[0], args[1]),
   233	  "hooks-trusted": () => hooksTrusted(args[0], args[1], args[2], args.slice(3)),
   234	};
   235	if (!commands[cmd]) {
   236	  console.error(`usage: inspect.mjs ${Object.keys(commands).join("|")} ...`);
   237	  process.exit(2);
   238	}
   239	let ok = false;
   240	try {
   241	  ok = commands[cmd]();
   242	} catch (err) {
   243	  console.log(`inspect.mjs ${cmd}: ${err.message}`);
   244	}
   245	process.exit(ok ? 0 : 1);

AGENTS.md-1-# Working in this repo
AGENTS.md-2-
AGENTS.md-3-This repo is a Claude Code and Codex plugin marketplace. `adhd-unslop`
AGENTS.md:4:combines the i-have-adhd and unslop skills with one tie-breaker and ships
AGENTS.md-5-everything it needs. `au-i-have-adhd` and `au-unslop` are optional pinned
AGENTS.md:6:mirrors of the two upstream skills. Read `design/DECISIONS.md` before
AGENTS.md-7-changing structure. It records why each part is the way it is and the
AGENTS.md-8-platform behavior already verified, so nothing needs researching again.
AGENTS.md-9-
AGENTS.md-10-There is no `CLAUDE.md`. Claude Code reads `AGENTS.md` when no `CLAUDE.md`
--
AGENTS.md-12-this file from Claude Code.
AGENTS.md-13-
AGENTS.md-14-## Edit sources, never generated files
AGENTS.md-15-
AGENTS.md:16:- Edit `src/`, `tools/plugins.json`, and `tools/upstream.json`. Every file
AGENTS.md-17-  under `plugins/`, and both marketplace files, is generated.
AGENTS.md-18-- Run `node tools/build.mjs`, then `node --test tests/*.test.mjs`. CI fails
AGENTS.md-19-  on a stale generated file and on any stray file under `plugins/`,
AGENTS.md-20-  `.claude-plugin/`, or `.agents/plugins/`. `node tools/build.mjs --prune`
AGENTS.md-21-  deletes strays.
AGENTS.md:22:- Change upstream text only through `node tools/sync.mjs --bump <name>
AGENTS.md:23:  <commit>` or the daily workflow. Never edit `upstream/` by hand.
AGENTS.md-24-
AGENTS.md-25-## Rules that are easy to break
AGENTS.md-26-
AGENTS.md-27-- Raise the version in `tools/plugins.json` for every plugin whose shipped
--
AGENTS.md-33-  Codex user back to `/hooks`. `tests/hook.test.mjs` compares them with
AGENTS.md-34-  `tests/fixtures/trusted-session-start-group.json`. Put behavior changes in
AGENTS.md-35-  `always-on.mjs` or `lib.mjs` instead. If a handler must change, update the
AGENTS.md-36-  fixture on purpose and say so in the release notes.
AGENTS.md:37:- Cite upstream items only as "ADHD rule N", "ADHD exception N", "ADHD
AGENTS.md-38-  check N", "unslop rule N", or "unslop process N". `sync.mjs` checks these
AGENTS.md:39:  labels against upstream. Other wording escapes the check.
AGENTS.md-40-- Keep overlay prose to unslop's rules. `tests/overlay.test.mjs` checks for
AGENTS.md-41-  em dashes, curly quotes, title-case headings, and rule 7 words.
AGENTS.md-42-- Hooks must exit 0 on every path and never block session start.
AGENTS.md:43:- No plugin `dependencies`. A plugin that needs an upstream text embeds it
AGENTS.md:44:  with a `{{upstream <name>}}` line in its skill template and lists it in
AGENTS.md:45:  `upstreams` in `tools/plugins.json` (DECISIONS D4, D9). Never make a skill
AGENTS.md-46-  read rule text from a file: Claude Code prompts for the read and Codex
AGENTS.md-47-  often skips it.
AGENTS.md-48-- Only `adhd-unslop` has an always-on hook. A second one would inject the
AGENTS.md-49-  ADHD rules twice.
AGENTS.md:50:- Codex skill names are `$plugin:skill`, such as
AGENTS.md-51-  `$adhd-unslop:adhd-unslop`. A bare name does not match. Never suggest a
AGENTS.md:52:  `~/.agents/skills` symlink, because it creates a duplicate name that
AGENTS.md-53-  Codex refuses to load.
AGENTS.md-54-
AGENTS.md-55-## Adding a plugin
AGENTS.md-56-
AGENTS.md-57-1. Add an entry to `tools/plugins.json`: name, version, `"kind":
AGENTS.md-58-   "authored"`, category, descriptions, keywords, `codexInterface`, and
AGENTS.md:59:   `skills`, with `upstreams` for any upstream a skill embeds.
AGENTS.md:60:2. Write `src/<plugin>/skills/<skill>/SKILL.md`, or a `SKILL.md.tmpl` whose
AGENTS.md-61-   `{{include <path>}}` lines pull files from `src/<plugin>/` and whose
AGENTS.md:62:   `{{upstream <name>}}` lines embed pinned upstream text, plus
AGENTS.md-63-   `agents/openai.yaml` for its Codex policy.
AGENTS.md-64-3. Run the build and the tests, then `node tools/load-check.mjs`.
AGENTS.md-65-
AGENTS.md-66-## Testing with the real CLIs
--
AGENTS.md-68-- `node tools/load-check.mjs` installs the marketplace into throwaway
AGENTS.md-69-  Claude Code and Codex homes with no keys and checks what loads. CI runs
AGENTS.md-70-  it with the versions pinned in `tools/cli-versions.json`, and daily
AGENTS.md-71-  against the latest releases.
AGENTS.md:72:- Run `tests/e2e/run.sh` before merging a change to skills, hooks, or
AGENTS.md-73-  manifests. It drives the models, so it needs signed-in CLIs.
AGENTS.md-74-  `tests/e2e/run.sh migrate` checks the upgrade from 0.2.2.
AGENTS.md-75-  `E2E_REPO=<owner/repo> E2E_REF=<branch>` installs from GitHub, which
AGENTS.md:76:  tests the real cache paths.
AGENTS.md-77-- For any ad hoc test, use a throwaway home: `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR=<dir>` for
AGENTS.md-78-  Claude Code, and `HOME=<dir> CODEX_HOME=<dir>/.codex` for Codex, with a
AGENTS.md-79-  copy of `~/.codex/auth.json` that you delete afterwards. Delete the whole
AGENTS.md-80-  Codex home when done: Codex writes the shell environment, API keys
--
AGENTS.md-94-- For a design change, write the proposal under `design/` and run Codex
AGENTS.md-95-  adversarial review rounds, `design/round-N-prompt.md` and
AGENTS.md-96-  `round-N-codex.md`, until the verdict is CONSENSUS. Record the result in
AGENTS.md-97-  `design/DECISIONS.md`.
AGENTS.md:98:- Review each `upstream-bump` PR for new conflicts between the two skills.
AGENTS.md-99-  Record any new conflict in the outcome table in
AGENTS.md-100-  `src/adhd-unslop/overlay/10-precedence.md`.
AGENTS.md-101-- After a merge, installed copies update per plugin. The README lists the
AGENTS.md-102-  commands for each runtime.

README.md-6-tie-breaker.
README.md-7-
README.md-8-| Plugin | Skills | What it is |
README.md-9-| --- | --- | --- |
README.md:10:| `adhd-unslop` | `adhd-unslop`, `doctor` | The overlay rules plus both upstream texts, an always-on hook, and a doctor. Everything you need. |
README.md-11-| `au-i-have-adhd` | `i-have-adhd` | Optional. A pinned, unchanged copy of ayghri/i-have-adhd |
README.md:12:| `au-unslop` | `unslop` | Optional. A pinned, unchanged copy of the unslop skill from pstack-claude |
README.md-13-
README.md:14:All upstream text comes from copies pinned by commit and sha256 in
README.md:15:`tools/upstream.json`. The `au-` prefix keeps the mirrors from clashing with
README.md:16:the upstream plugins of the same name.
README.md-17-
README.md-18-## The rule
README.md-19-
README.md-20-Both rule sets stay active. When a rule from each would produce different text
--
README.md-23-- In a direct reply to the user, i-have-adhd wins.
README.md-24-- In all other writing, unslop wins. This includes files, docs, commit
README.md-25-  messages, PR text, subagent prompts, and text you were asked to rewrite.
README.md-26-
README.md:27:Compatible rules apply everywhere. The skill defines both surfaces per
README.md-28-passage, lists the known interactions in an outcome table, and adds
README.md-29-independent off switches. The overlay text is in `src/adhd-unslop/overlay/`.
README.md-30-
README.md-31-## Install
--
README.md-37-claude plugin install adhd-unslop@adhd-unslop
README.md-38-```
README.md-39-
README.md-40-Type `/adhd-unslop` in a session. If another command uses that name, type
README.md:41:`/adhd-unslop:adhd-unslop`. The skill carries both upstream texts itself. It
README.md-42-has `disable-model-invocation: true`, so nothing applies until you invoke it
README.md-43-or turn on always-on mode.
README.md-44-
README.md-45-### Codex
--
README.md-48-codex plugin marketplace add rubenvarela/adhd-unslop --ref main
README.md-49-codex plugin add adhd-unslop@adhd-unslop
README.md-50-```
README.md-51-
README.md:52:Type `$adhd-unslop:adhd-unslop` in a session. Codex matches plugin skills by
README.md-53-their full name, so a bare `$adhd-unslop` finds nothing.
README.md-54-
README.md:55:Do not link the skill into `~/.agents/skills`. Codex names a linked skill
README.md-56-after the plugin that contains it, so the link becomes a second
README.md:57:`adhd-unslop:adhd-unslop`, and with two skills of one name Codex loads
README.md-58-neither. The doctor warns about such a link.
README.md-59-
README.md-60-### The optional mirrors
README.md-61-
README.md:62:Install `au-i-have-adhd` or `au-unslop` only if you want that skill on its
README.md-63-own. `adhd-unslop` never needs them.
README.md-64-
README.md-65-```bash
README.md-66-claude plugin install au-i-have-adhd@adhd-unslop     # then /au-i-have-adhd:i-have-adhd
README.md-67-codex plugin add au-unslop@adhd-unslop               # then $au-unslop:unslop
README.md-68-```
README.md-69-
README.md:70:They behave exactly as upstream ships them: i-have-adhd runs only when you
README.md-71-type it, and unslop can also be chosen by the model. With the unslop mirror
README.md-72-installed, Codex sometimes loads it on its own. That copy is separate from
README.md-73-`adhd-unslop`, so `stop unslop` does not turn it off. If you use
README.md-74-`adhd-unslop`, you do not need the unslop mirror.

README.md-105-

README.md:107:the skill:
README.md-108-
README.md-109-```bash
README.md-110-touch ~/.claude/.adhd-unslop-always      # or "$CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR/.adhd-unslop-always"
README.md-111-touch ~/.codex/.adhd-unslop-always       # or "$CODEX_HOME/.adhd-unslop-always"
--
README.md-144-  always-on already delivered the rules adds a second, identical copy.
README.md-145-
README.md-146-### How the always-on hook works
README.md-147-
README.md:148:The hook carries its own copy of both upstream texts, so it works without the
README.md-149-mirrors and without the model reading any file first.
README.md-150-
README.md-151-Claude Code caps each hook's output at 10,000 characters, and Codex allows
README.md-152-about 2,500 tokens per handler by default. The rules are about 21,500
--
README.md-172-
README.md-173-Type `/adhd-unslop:doctor` or `$adhd-unslop:doctor`. It prints one OK, WARN,
README.md-174-or FAIL line per check, with the fix for each problem, and changes nothing.
README.md-175-It checks the install, the optional mirrors, the always-on flags and switch,
README.md:176:the hook chunks, the Codex hooks feature, and a `~/.agents/skills` name
README.md-177-clash. You can also run it directly:
README.md-178-
README.md-179-```bash
README.md:180:node ~/.claude/plugins/cache/adhd-unslop/adhd-unslop/<version>/skills/doctor/scripts/doctor.mjs
README.md-181-```
README.md-182-
README.md-183-## Switches
README.md-184-
README.md-185-- `stop adhd mode` turns the ADHD rules off. unslop stays as it was.
README.md-186-- `stop unslop` turns the unslop rules off. ADHD stays as it was.
README.md-187-- `normal mode` turns both off.
README.md:188:- Invoking the skill again turns both on.
README.md-189-
README.md-190-Known limitations:
README.md-191-
README.md-192-- Mode state lives in the conversation. Compaction can lose it, and the
README.md-193-  re-injected instructions then restore the active defaults. Repeat the stop
README.md-194-  command if that happens.
README.md:195:- Without always-on, Claude Code keeps an invoked skill through `/compact`
README.md:196:  only up to 20,000 characters, and this skill is about 21,800, so the last
README.md-197-  unslop rules and the final check drop out. After a resume and a compaction
README.md-198-  it keeps none of it. For long sessions that compact, turn on always-on,
README.md-199-  which delivers the whole rule set again at every compaction, or invoke the
README.md:200:  skill again after compacting.
README.md-201-
README.md-202-## Upstream updates
README.md-203-
README.md:204:The `Bump upstream skills` workflow runs daily. It first runs
README.md-205-`node tools/sync.mjs --verify-remote`, which refetches each pinned file and
README.md-206-compares its hash. Then `node tools/sync.mjs --latest`:
README.md-207-
README.md:208:1. Finds the newest upstream commit that touched a pinned file.
README.md:209:2. Skips an upstream when that commit changed none of the pinned files.
README.md:210:3. Fetches the files and refuses a body that references files the plugins
README.md:211:   do not ship, such as `references/`, `scripts/`, or relative links.
README.md-212-4. Refuses a body that drops a rule, exception, check, or process step the
README.md-213-   overlay cites.
README.md-214-5. Swaps the files in, raises the patch version of each plugin that ships
README.md-215-   the text, rebuilds, and runs the tests. It restores everything if a step
README.md-216-   fails.
README.md-217-
README.md-218-The workflow opens or updates one PR with the results and starts the verify
README.md-219-workflow on it. When a bump fails, it opens an issue with the reason. The
README.md:220:usual cause is an upstream change to a rule the overlay cites, which needs an
README.md-221-overlay edit. It needs the repository setting "Allow GitHub Actions to create
README.md-222-and approve pull requests".
README.md-223-
README.md-224-To bump by hand:
README.md-225-
README.md-226-```bash
README.md:227:node tools/sync.mjs --check                        # upstream/ matches the pins
README.md-228-node tools/sync.mjs --bump i-have-adhd <commit>    # or: --bump unslop <commit>
README.md-229-git diff
README.md-230-```
README.md-231-
README.md:232:Review the upstream diff for conflicts with the other skill. Record them in
README.md-233-the outcome table in `src/adhd-unslop/overlay/10-precedence.md`.
README.md-234-

README.md-238-
README.md-239-```bash
README.md-240-node tools/build.mjs
README.md-241-node --test tests/*.test.mjs

design/STRUCTURE-v2.md-104-#### Embedding contract
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md-105-
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md:106:- A skill template embeds an upstream with a line `{{upstream <name>}}`.
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md:107:  The build replaces it with the upstream block: the line `<!-- BEGIN
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md:108:  upstream <name> <repo>/<path> @<commit> -->`, the upstream `SKILL.md`
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md-109-  with its frontmatter removed and a final newline ensured, and `<!-- END
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md:110:  upstream <name> -->`.
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md-111-- The always-on chunks use the same block, byte for byte.
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md:112:- `tools/plugins.json` lists each skill's embedded upstreams in
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md:113:  `upstreams`. The build fails when the template's directives and the list
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md:114:  disagree, or when a directive names an unpinned upstream.
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md:115:- Tests check that each listed block appears exactly once in the skill and
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md:116:  matches the hook chunk that carries it, and that the `adhd-unslop` skill
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md-117-  body equals the hook bundle.
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md-118-
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md-119-#### Versioning rule
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md-120-
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md:121:A plugin ships an upstream's text when it is that upstream's vendored
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md:122:mirror, when one of its skills embeds the upstream, or when its always-on
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md-123-chunks include it. `sync.mjs` raises the patch version of every such plugin
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md-124-once per bump run. A future `presentation` plugin that embeds i-have-adhd
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md-125-therefore gets a new version whenever i-have-adhd changes. The CI version
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md-126-gate catches every other change to shipped files.

design/STRUCTURE-v2.md-495-## Revision 5
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md-496-
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md-497-Revision 4 reached CONSENSUS in round 9. Implementing it, the end-to-end
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md-498-script (`design/research/11-tests-e2e.md`) found two failures in the
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md:499:`references/` mechanism of P1:
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md-500-
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md-501-1. Claude Code denied both reads in `-p` mode, because the files sit outside
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md:502:   the working directory. The model answered anyway, with no upstream rules
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md-503-   loaded. An interactive session would ask for permission on each read.
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md-504-2. Codex read the files in 1 of 8 runs with a short prompt, and in 4 of 4
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md-505-   with the plugin's own default prompt. Whether the rules load depended on
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md-506-   the prompt.
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md-507-
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md:508:Revision 5 changes only how the skill gets the texts. It embeds them with a
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md:509:`{{upstream <name>}}` template directive, so the skill body is the hook
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md-510-bundle byte for byte. Everything else in revision 4 stands: no plugin
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md-511-dependencies, byte-for-byte mirrors, the frozen hook handlers, the launcher's
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md-512-resume rule, the generated `plugins/`, the doctor, and CI.
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md-513-
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md-514-It also records two findings from the same run:
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md-515-
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md-516-- With both mirrors installed, Codex 0.154.0 loaded `au-unslop:unslop` for
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md-517-  an unrelated question in 2 of 4 runs, and 0.157.1 in 0 of 4. That is
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md:518:  upstream's frontmatter, kept on purpose by P2. A user with always-on and
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md-519-  this mirror gets a second unslop that `stop unslop` does not control. The
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md:520:  README says so, and the check for an unprompted skill load now runs
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md-521-  before the mirrors are installed.
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md-522-- A mirror kept by name in Claude Code stays at its old version after the
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md-523-  upgrade until `claude plugin update` runs on it. P10 now says so.
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md-524-
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md-525-The second end-to-end run on revision 5 passed every check: Claude Code 17
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md-526-of 17, Codex 0.154.0 19 of 19, Codex 0.157.1 19 of 19, and `migrate` 16 of
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md:527:16 in each runtime. Both runtimes received the whole skill body byte for
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md:528:byte, so Codex does not cut a skill of about 22 KB.
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md-529-
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md-530-It also measured one platform limit (`11`, last section). With always-on
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md:531:off, Claude Code 2.1.283 re-attaches an invoked skill after `/compact` cut
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md-532-to 20,000 characters, which drops unslop rules 32 and 33, the END line, and
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md-533-the final check. After `--resume` and then `/compact`, it re-attaches
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md-534-nothing. The always-on hook is not affected, because it injects the whole
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md-535-bundle again on `compact`.
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md-536-
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md:537:Revision 5 documents this instead of reshaping the skill:
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md-538-
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md-539-- Shrinking the body under 20,000 characters would fix only the first case,
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md-540-  and would mean cutting overlay text that five review rounds settled, with
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md:541:  upstream growth pushing it over again later.
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md:542:- Moving the final check ahead of the upstream texts would lose other rules
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md:543:  instead, and would break the identity between the skill body and the hook
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md-544-  bundle, because chunk 1 has no room for it under the 10,000-character cap.
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md-545-- The README tells users who work in long, compacting sessions to turn on
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md:546:  always-on, or to invoke the skill again after a compaction. A unit test
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md:547:  reports the skill's size against the 20,000-character re-attach budget, so
design/STRUCTURE-v2.md-548-  the gap stays visible.

   180	const snake = (event) => event.replace(/([a-z0-9])([A-Z])/g, "$1_$2").toLowerCase();
   181	
   182	function listFiles(dir) {
   183	  const out = [];
   184	  if (!fs.existsSync(dir)) return out;
   185	  for (const entry of fs.readdirSync(dir, { withFileTypes: true })) {
   186	    const abs = path.join(dir, entry.name);
   187	    if (entry.isDirectory()) out.push(...listFiles(abs));
   188	    else out.push(abs);
   189	  }
   190	  return out;
   191	}

   193	function expectations(copy) {
   194	  const config = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(path.join(copy, "tools", "plugins.json"), "utf8"));
   195	  const mkt = config.marketplace.name;
   196	  const plugins = config.plugins.map((p) => {
   197	    const root = path.join(copy, "plugins", p.name);
   198	    const skills = p.kind === "vendored" ? [p.skill] : Object.keys(p.skills ?? {});
   199	    const required = [];

   201	    // The hooks file Codex reads, as the Codex manifest names it, relative to the plugin root.
   202	    let hooksFile = "hooks/hooks.json";
   203	    try {
   204	      const codexManifest = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(path.join(root, ".codex-plugin", "plugin.json"), "utf8"));
   205	      if (typeof codexManifest.hooks === "string") hooksFile = codexManifest.hooks.replace(/^\.\//, "");
   206	    } catch { /* checked by the install steps */ }
   207	    const hookKeys = [];
   208	    const hooksPath = path.join(root, hooksFile);
   209	    if (fs.existsSync(hooksPath)) {
   210	      required.push(hooksFile);
   211	      const hooks = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(hooksPath, "utf8")).hooks ?? {};
   212	      for (const [event, groups] of Object.entries(hooks)) {
   213	        groups.forEach((group, g) => (group.hooks ?? []).forEach((_, h) => hookKeys.push(`${p.name}@${mkt}:${hooksFile}:${snake(event)}:${g}:${h}`)));

   215	    }
   216	    if (p.alwaysOn) {

   218	      p.alwaysOn.forEach((_, i) => required.push(`hooks/chunks/${i + 1}.md`));
   219	    }

   327	### P9. CI
   328	
   329	`verify.yml`, renamed from `daily-build.yml`, runs on pull requests, pushes
   330	to `main`, and manual dispatch.
   331	
   332	- `checks`: `sync.mjs --check`, `build.mjs --check` with the stray-file
   333	  rule, unit tests, and on pull requests a version gate. The gate fails when
   334	  files under `plugins/<name>/` differ from the merge base and that plugin's
   335	  version did not rise. The pattern comes from expo.
   336	- `load`: installs pinned Claude Code and Codex versions and runs
   337	  `tools/load-check.mjs` with no keys, in throwaway homes.
   338	  - Claude Code: `validate --strict` on the marketplace and each plugin,
   339	    `marketplace add` of the checkout, `install adhd-unslop@adhd-unslop`,
   340	    and `list --json`. It asserts that only `adhd-unslop` installs, with the
   341	    expected version and no errors, and that its cache copy holds the skill,
   342	    both reference files, and the hooks.
   343	  - Codex: `codex plugin marketplace add` of the checkout and `codex plugin
   344	    add adhd-unslop@adhd-unslop`. It asserts that only `adhd-unslop` is in
   345	    the cache, and checks its cache copy the same way.
   346	  - Both runtimes, second step: install `au-i-have-adhd` and `au-unslop` by
   347	    name, and assert that each installs with its expected version and skill.
   348	  - Codex, catalog: `app-server` with `initialize`, `plugin/list`, and
   349	    `plugin/read`. `plugin/read` reads the marketplace entry, installed or
   350	    not, so it asserts full skill names, hook keys, and versions for all
   351	    three plugins. The pattern comes from trailofbits.
   352	- `workflows`: actionlint and zizmor, from trailofbits and gstack.
   495	## Revision 5
   496	
   497	Revision 4 reached CONSENSUS in round 9. Implementing it, the end-to-end
   498	script (`design/research/11-tests-e2e.md`) found two failures in the
   499	`references/` mechanism of P1:
   500	
   501	1. Claude Code denied both reads in `-p` mode, because the files sit outside
   502	   the working directory. The model answered anyway, with no upstream rules
   503	   loaded. An interactive session would ask for permission on each read.
   504	2. Codex read the files in 1 of 8 runs with a short prompt, and in 4 of 4
   505	   with the plugin's own default prompt. Whether the rules load depended on
   506	   the prompt.
   507	
   508	Revision 5 changes only how the skill gets the texts. It embeds them with a
   509	`{{upstream <name>}}` template directive, so the skill body is the hook
   510	bundle byte for byte. Everything else in revision 4 stands: no plugin
   511	dependencies, byte-for-byte mirrors, the frozen hook handlers, the launcher's
   512	resume rule, the generated `plugins/`, the doctor, and CI.
   513	
   514	It also records two findings from the same run:
   515	
   516	- With both mirrors installed, Codex 0.154.0 loaded `au-unslop:unslop` for
   517	  an unrelated question in 2 of 4 runs, and 0.157.1 in 0 of 4. That is
   518	  upstream's frontmatter, kept on purpose by P2. A user with always-on and
   519	  this mirror gets a second unslop that `stop unslop` does not control. The
   520	  README says so, and the check for an unprompted skill load now runs
   521	  before the mirrors are installed.
   522	- A mirror kept by name in Claude Code stays at its old version after the
   523	  upgrade until `claude plugin update` runs on it. P10 now says so.
   524	
   525	The second end-to-end run on revision 5 passed every check: Claude Code 17
   526	of 17, Codex 0.154.0 19 of 19, Codex 0.157.1 19 of 19, and `migrate` 16 of
   527	16 in each runtime. Both runtimes received the whole skill body byte for
   528	byte, so Codex does not cut a skill of about 22 KB.
   529	
   530	It also measured one platform limit (`11`, last section). With always-on
   531	off, Claude Code 2.1.283 re-attaches an invoked skill after `/compact` cut
   532	to 20,000 characters, which drops unslop rules 32 and 33, the END line, and
   533	the final check. After `--resume` and then `/compact`, it re-attaches
   534	nothing. The always-on hook is not affected, because it injects the whole
   535	bundle again on `compact`.
   536	
   537	Revision 5 documents this instead of reshaping the skill:
   538	
   539	- Shrinking the body under 20,000 characters would fix only the first case,
   540	  and would mean cutting overlay text that five review rounds settled, with
   541	  upstream growth pushing it over again later.
   542	- Moving the final check ahead of the upstream texts would lose other rules
   543	  instead, and would break the identity between the skill body and the hook
   544	  bundle, because chunk 1 has no room for it under the 10,000-character cap.
   545	- The README tells users who work in long, compacting sessions to turn on
   546	  always-on, or to invoke the skill again after a compaction. A unit test
   547	  reports the skill's size against the 20,000-character re-attach budget, so
   548	  the gap stays visible.
   116	### D7. Vendored mirrors keep the upstream frontmatter
   117	
   118	The mirrors ship the upstream `SKILL.md` unchanged. The build generates
   119	`agents/openai.yaml` so Codex matches Claude Code: `allow_implicit_invocation:
   120	false` when the upstream sets `disable-model-invocation: true`, `true`
   121	otherwise. Neither upstream skill folder has its own `openai.yaml`.
   122	
   123	- Result: i-have-adhd stays user-only and unslop stays model-invocable, as
   124	  upstream ships them. Typed invocation works in both runtimes.
   125	- History: 0.2.x rewrote the frontmatter so `adhd-unslop` could load the
   126	  mirrors through the Skill tool. Nothing loads them now.
   127	
   128	### D8. The composed skill runs only when asked
   129	
   130	`adhd-unslop` keeps `disable-model-invocation: true` and
   131	`allow_implicit_invocation: false`. It runs on explicit invocation or
   132	through the always-on hook. The doctor skill has the same settings.
   133	
   134	### D9. The adhd-unslop skill embeds the upstream texts
   135	
   136	The skill's body, after a one-line generated comment, is the always-on
   137	bundle byte for byte: the overlay sections, the i-have-adhd block, the
   138	unslop block, and the final check. It reads no files.
   139	
   140	- Why: a first 0.3.0 build had the skill read the texts from `references/`
   141	  files next to it. Claude Code denied both reads in `-p` mode, because the
   142	  files sit outside the working directory, and an interactive session would
   143	  ask for permission each time. Codex skipped the reads in 7 of 8 runs with
   144	  a short prompt (`research/11`). Rules that must always apply cannot
   145	  depend on a permission rule or on the model choosing to read a file.
   146	  `references/` suits optional detail. These texts are the rules.
   147	- Rejected: `allowed-tools: Read` in the frontmatter. It lets Claude Code
   148	  read without asking but pre-approves every Read while the skill is active,
   149	  and does nothing for Codex.
   150	- Embedding contract: a `{{upstream <name>}}` template line becomes the
   151	  BEGIN line, the upstream body with its frontmatter removed and a final
   152	  newline, and the END line. The hook chunks carry the same bytes.
   153	- Size: about 21,800 bytes. Codex does not cut it. Codex cuts at 8,000 bytes
   154	  only for a plugin with a root `plugin.json` that uses the agent-plugins
   155	  `$schema` (`research/07` C1). The end-to-end check confirms the whole
   156	  skill arrives in both runtimes.
   157	- A user who invokes the skill while always-on already delivered the bundle
   158	  gets a second, identical copy. The bundle rules treat it as a repeat.
   159	- Compaction limit: without always-on, Claude Code 2.1.283 re-attaches an
   160	  invoked skill after `/compact` cut to 20,000 characters, which drops the
   161	  last unslop rules and the final check, and after `--resume` then
   162	  `/compact` it re-attaches nothing (`research/11`). The always-on hook
   163	  injects the whole bundle again on `compact`, so the README points long,
   164	  compacting sessions at always-on. Shrinking the skill would fix only the
   165	  first case and would cut settled overlay text. A unit test reports the
   166	  size against the 20,000-character budget.
   167	- History: 0.2.x loaded the mirrors through the Skill tool. 0.1.0 embedded
   168	  the texts, as 0.3.0 does again.
    60	### The optional mirrors
    61	
    62	Install `au-i-have-adhd` or `au-unslop` only if you want that skill on its
    63	own. `adhd-unslop` never needs them.
    64	
    65	```bash
    66	claude plugin install au-i-have-adhd@adhd-unslop     # then /au-i-have-adhd:i-have-adhd
    67	codex plugin add au-unslop@adhd-unslop               # then $au-unslop:unslop
    68	```
    69	
    70	They behave exactly as upstream ships them: i-have-adhd runs only when you
    71	type it, and unslop can also be chosen by the model. With the unslop mirror
    72	installed, Codex sometimes loads it on its own. That copy is separate from
    73	`adhd-unslop`, so `stop unslop` does not turn it off. If you use
    74	`adhd-unslop`, you do not need the unslop mirror.
    75	
   130	### When the rules arrive
   131	
   132	The hook runs at `startup`, `clear`, `compact`, and `resume`.
   133	
   134	- Claude Code keeps the earlier copy in a resumed session, so the hook adds
   135	  nothing on resume. If you turn always-on on in the middle of a session,
   136	  start a new session to get the rules.
   137	- Codex also keeps the earlier copy, but it can lose it: after a manual
   138	  compaction, Codex fires the hook only when your next message starts. If
   139	  you compact and quit before sending a message, the resumed session would
   140	  have no copy. So the hook injects on every Codex resume, which can leave a
   141	  second copy in context.
   142	- If the rules ever seem missing, invoke `/adhd-unslop` or
   143	  `$adhd-unslop:adhd-unslop`, or run the doctor. Invoking it while
   144	  always-on already delivered the rules adds a second, identical copy.
   145	
   185	- `stop adhd mode` turns the ADHD rules off. unslop stays as it was.
   186	- `stop unslop` turns the unslop rules off. ADHD stays as it was.
   187	- `normal mode` turns both off.
   188	- Invoking the skill again turns both on.
   189	
   190	Known limitations:
   191	
   192	- Mode state lives in the conversation. Compaction can lose it, and the
   193	  re-injected instructions then restore the active defaults. Repeat the stop
   194	  command if that happens.
   195	- Without always-on, Claude Code keeps an invoked skill through `/compact`
   196	  only up to 20,000 characters, and this skill is about 21,800, so the last
   197	  unslop rules and the final check drop out. After a resume and a compaction
   198	  it keeps none of it. For long sessions that compact, turn on always-on,
   199	  which delivers the whole rule set again at every compaction, or invoke the
   200	  skill again after compacting.
   201

   239	// ---------------------------------------------------------------- cache checks
   240	
   241	const dirNames = (dir) => (fs.existsSync(dir) ? fs.readdirSync(dir).filter((n) => n !== ".DS_Store").sort() : []);
   242	
   243	// <cache>/<mkt>/ holds exactly the installed plugins, each with one version folder.
   244	function checkCacheLayout(label, mktCache, installed) {
   245	  const names = dirNames(mktCache);
   246	  check(`${label}: cache ${path.basename(mktCache)}/ holds only ${show(installed.map((p) => p.name))}`, sameSet(names, installed.map((p) => p.name)), `found: ${show(names)}`);
   247	  for (const p of installed) {
   248	    const versions = dirNames(path.join(mktCache, p.name));
   249	    check(`${label}: cache ${p.name}/ holds only ${p.version}/`, sameSet(versions, [p.version]), `found: ${show(versions)}`);
   250	  }
   251	}
   252	
   253	// The installed copy has the required files, and every shipped file byte for byte.
   254	// Extra files are allowed, so a CLI that adds metadata does not fail the check.
   255	function checkInstalledFiles(label, p, dir) {
   256	  const missing = p.required.filter((rel) => !fs.existsSync(path.join(dir, rel)));
   257	  check(`${label}: ${p.name} install has ${p.required.length} required files`, missing.length === 0, `missing under ${dir}:\n${missing.join("\n")}`);
   258	  const differ = p.sourceFiles.filter((rel) => {
   259	    const out = path.join(dir, rel);
   260	    return !fs.existsSync(out) || !fs.readFileSync(out).equals(fs.readFileSync(path.join(p.root, rel)));
   261	  });
   262	  check(`${label}: ${p.name} install matches all ${p.sourceFiles.length} shipped files`, p.sourceFiles.length > 0 && differ.length === 0, `missing or different under ${dir}:\n${differ.join("\n") || "(no shipped files found)"}`);
   263	}
   264	
   265	// ---------------------------------------------------------------- Claude Code
   266	
   267	function parseJson(text, what) {
   268	  const trimmed = text.trim();
   269	  const start = [trimmed.indexOf("["), trimmed.indexOf("{")].filter((i) => i >= 0).sort((a, b) => a - b)[0];
   270	  try {
   271	    return JSON.parse(start > 0 ? trimmed.slice(start) : trimmed);
   272	  } catch (err) {
   273	    throw new Error(`${what}: not JSON (${err.message}):\n${tail(text)}`);
   274	  }
   275	}
   276	
   277	function claudeList(bin, opts, label) {
   278	  const r = step(`${label}: claude plugin list --json`, bin, ["plugin", "list", "--json"], opts);
   279	  const list = parseJson(r.stdout, "claude plugin list --json");
   280	  if (!Array.isArray(list)) throw new Error(`claude plugin list --json: expected an array, got ${typeof list}`);
   281	  return list;
   282	}
   283	
   284	function checkClaudeInstalled(label, list, expected, mkt, configDir) {
   285	  const ours = list.filter((e) => String(e.id).endsWith(`@${mkt}`));
   286	  check(`${label}: installed from ${mkt}: exactly ${show(expected.map((p) => p.name))}`, sameSet(ours.map((e) => e.id), expected.map((p) => p.id)), `installed: ${show(ours.map((e) => e.id))}`);
   287	  for (const p of expected) {
   288	    const entry = ours.find((e) => e.id === p.id);
   289	    if (!entry) continue;

./design/round-1-log.txt:879:pstack-claude--repo/README.md-45-`plugins/pstack/skills` is a supported installation boundary. Everything a skill reads at runtime lives inside it, including the `poteto-agent` and `comment-sicko` definitions under `poteto-mode/references/agents/` and the MIT terms under `poteto-mode/references/licenses/`. Both directories sit inside a skill because the CLI installs skill directories and drops loose files at the tree root. The generator stamps exactly five portable assets from the sources declared in `PORTABLE_ASSETS` and removes stale output from those two generated directories. It rejects missing or escaping local Markdown links and direct instructions to open paths outside the skills tree. The `Skills-only install` CI job copies the tree with the CLI, compares every installed file with the source, and runs the same validation against the installed tree.
./design/round-1-log.txt:881:pstack-claude--repo/README.md:47:Three plugin features do not survive a skills-only install, because they belong to a specific runtime rather than to the skills. Claude Code's `SessionStart` auto-fire lives in `hooks/`, the Codex slash-command stubs live in `.codex-plugin/prompts/`, and Claude Code's native subagent registration reads `agents/`. On a skills-only install, dispatch `comment-sicko` by pointing the runtime's agent primitive at `poteto-mode/references/agents/comment-sicko.md`.
./design/round-1-log.txt:923:pstack-claude--repo/README.md-89-The Gemini path follows published Gemini CLI documentation and has not been run on a live session. Neither Gemini CLI nor opencode gets generated command files. Native discovery keeps each `SKILL.md`, its references, and its scripts attached to one installed directory, so the skills work from the repository where the user actually needs them.
./design/round-1-log.txt:925:pstack-claude--repo/README.md:91:Discovery is not a promise that Claude-specific execution details translate automatically. The skill bodies retain Claude Code tool names, `claude-*` model slugs, and Claude built-in skills. [`codex-tools.md`](plugins/pstack/skills/poteto-mode/references/codex-tools.md) maps those names on Codex only. Gemini CLI, opencode, and Prime Agent must use their own tool, model, and configuration equivalents. Delegation-heavy and multi-model workflows remain unverified on those runtimes. None has a plugin-hook equivalent of the Claude Code auto-fire hook, so ask the runtime to use `poteto-mode` explicitly.

---
name: unslop
description: Cut AI tells from any writing. Must always apply.
---

# Unslop

Edit text to remove AI patterns.

## Process

1. Scan for the patterns below.
2. Rewrite. Preserve meaning, match intended tone.

## Patterns to detect and fix

Rule numbers are stable ids that other skills cite. A removed rule leaves a gap.

### Content

3. **Superficial -ing phrases.** "highlighting...", "ensuring...", "reflecting...", "showcasing...", "fostering...". Delete or expand with real sources.
5. **Vague attributions.** "Experts believe", "Industry reports suggest", "Some critics argue". Name the source or delete.

### Language

7. **AI vocabulary.** Additionally, crucial, delve, enduring, enhance, fostering, garner, interplay, intricate, landscape (abstract), pivotal, showcase, tapestry (abstract), testament, underscore, vibrant. Replace with plain words.
8. **Fancy ways to say "is".** "serves as", "stands as", "boasts", "features". Just say "is" or "has".
9. **"Not just X, but Y."** State the point directly instead.
10. **Rule of three.** Forcing ideas into groups of three. Use the natural number.
11. **Synonym cycling.** Protagonist, main character, central figure, hero all in one paragraph. Pick one, repeat it.
12. **False ranges.** "from X to Y" where X and Y aren't on a meaningful scale. List topics directly.

### Style

13. **Em dash overuse.** Avoid em dashes entirely. Use periods or commas only (no parentheses, no en dashes, no hyphen-as-dash substitutes). If a thought needs separation, end the sentence or use a comma.
14. **Colon overuse.** Colons are fine before a list or example. Not as mid-sentence connectors. "If you're coming from traditional automation: instead of registering event handlers, you describe conditions" adds nothing with the colon. Rewrite to let the point stand on its own without comparison framing. "Describing when the scheduler should fire works best as plain English." Same meaning, no crutch punctuation.
15. **Boldface overuse.** Don't bold every proper noun or acronym.
16. **Inline-header lists.** The tell is a bold label and colon that restates the line: "**Performance:** Performance improved...". Convert those to prose. A bold lead-in that ends in a period, names the item, and is followed by genuinely new detail ("**Schema in TypeScript.** Tables live in one file.") is fine, not a tell.
17. **Title case headings.** Use sentence case.
18. **Decorative emojis.** Remove from headings and bullets.
19. **Curly quotes.** Replace with straight quotes.

### Communication artifacts

20. **Chatbot phrases.** "I hope this helps!", "Let me know if...", "Of course!", "Certainly!", "Found the smoking gun!" Remove.
22. **Sycophantic tone.** "Great question! You're absolutely right!" Respond directly.

### Filler

23. **Filler phrases.** "In order to" becomes "To". "Due to the fact that" becomes "Because". "It is important to note that" gets deleted.
24. **Excessive hedging.** "could potentially possibly be argued that it might" becomes "may".
25. **Generic conclusions.** "The future looks bright." State specific plans or facts.

### Jargon

26. **Abstract metaphor nouns.** Substrate, wedge, vector, locus, vantage, nexus, primitive (as noun), harness (as metaphor), surface (as in "API surface"), bedrock, scaffolding (as metaphor), modality, paradigm, gold-plating, ratchet (as metaphor), evacuate (for moving code), endgame, north star, flywheel. These read as technical but usually have a plainer concrete word. "Substrate" becomes "base". "Wedge in" becomes "add". "Vector" becomes "way" or "method". "Gold-plating" becomes "more than the job needs". "Ratchet" becomes the mechanism's real name or "a limit that only tightens". "Evacuate" becomes "move out". "Endgame" becomes "the last phase". Pick the concrete word.

### Plain speech

27. **Say what it does, not how it feels.** "the database stays close at hand", "SQL you can read", "types that follow your schema" name a feeling. The fix names the mechanism or a number: "`.toSQL()` returns the exact string sent to the database", "a column rename fails the build". Ask what the sentence tells the reader to do or know, then write that. If you can't restate it as a concrete instruction, fact, or number, cut it. One more check: if the sentence could appear unchanged in another project's docs, it says nothing about this one. Cut it.
28. **Shorten or split dense sentences.** If the reader has to backtrack to parse a sentence, break it in two or drop clauses. One idea per sentence.
29. **Active voice.** Prefer it. Catch "is/are/was/were + past participle" and name the actor: "queries are validated" becomes "the compiler validates queries", "the file is parsed by the loader" becomes "the loader parses the file". Passive is fine only when the actor is unknown or genuinely doesn't matter.
30. **Cut adverbs, or use a stronger verb.** "runs quickly" becomes "is fast" or the number. "significantly improves" becomes the measured delta. An adverb propping up a weak verb means the verb is wrong.
31. **Prefer the plain word.** "utilize" becomes "use", "leverage" becomes "use", "facilitate" becomes "help", "numerous" becomes "many", "in the event that" becomes "if". The fancier synonym is rarely clearer.
32. **Mannered prose.** Metaphor or flourish where a literal phrase exists: aphorisms ("wire it or delete it"), rhetorical fragments for effect, personified code ("the plan holds it"), figurative verbs ("rides along", "stands on"), stock framing phrases. "A dial worth turning" becomes "a parameter worth varying". Say what you mean. Rule 26 covers the metaphor nouns.
33. **Over-compression.** Dropped articles, verbless fragments, symbol-speak, and abbreviations that make the reader decode instead of read. "Parser rejects bad date → exit 2, no write" becomes "The parser rejects a bad date, exits with code 2, and writes nothing." Write whole sentences with their articles and verbs, and spell out arrows and abbreviations.
---
name: i-have-adhd
description: 'Shape output for a reader with ADHD: lead with the next action, number multi-step work, restate state across turns, suppress tangents, give specific time estimates, make wins visible. Invoke with /i-have-adhd; stays on until "stop adhd mode".'
disable-model-invocation: true
license: MIT
metadata:
  tags: "ADHD, Output Style, Productivity, Formatting"
  category: "productivity"

# i-have-adhd

The reader has ADHD. Output is not just brief. It is shaped so an ADHD brain can act on it.

## Persistence

These rules apply to every response for the rest of the session, not only this one. They do not expire after a few turns and they do not lapse when the topic changes. If you are unsure whether they still apply, they do.

Turn them off only when the reader says "stop adhd mode" or "normal mode". Confirm in one line, then return to your default style.

## What ADHD changes about reading

Five facts drive every rule below:

1. Working memory is small. Anything not on screen is forgotten. Do not ask the reader to "keep in mind X."
2. Knowing the answer is not doing the answer. The friction between "got it" and "done it" is where work dies.
3. Starting is the hardest step. The first action must be obvious, small, and doable now.
4. Time estimates feel uniform. "A bit of work" and "a few hours" register the same. Vague estimates fail.
5. Dopamine is scarce. Visible progress matters. Buried wins do not register.

## Rules

### 1. Lead with the next action

The first line is something the reader can do. Not context. Not a plan. The action.

Bad: "Let's think about this. Your auth flow has a few moving pieces..."
Good: "Run `npm install jsonwebtoken`, then edit `src/auth.ts:42`."

If the answer is a command, path, or snippet, it goes first. Prose comes after, if at all.

### 2. Number multi-step tasks

If the work takes more than one step, write a numbered list. Each step is one bounded action. No step contains "and then" twice.

Use the fewest steps that still work. Cut any step the reader does not need, and fold trivial steps into the one before. A short path finished beats a complete path abandoned.

Bad: "First open the file, find the function, swap it out, then run the tests."

Good:
```
1. Open `src/auth.ts`
2. Replace `verifyToken` (lines 42 to 58) with the snippet below
3. Run `npm test -- auth.spec.ts`
```

     8

    10	2. An incomplete bundle initializes neither mode.

    14

    23	
    24	1. Restore the latest known state of each mode in this session.
    25	2. Explicit invocation enables both modes.
    26	3. Stop commands change only the named mode.
    27	4. Receiving or reloading these instructions preserves known states.
    28	5. Once a complete matching bundle is available, each unknown mode defaults to active.

## Verdict

REVISE

## Answers

1. Embedding is the right reversal. The read failures are decisive, and build plus E2E checks prove the pinned blocks, hook bundle, and delivered skill body match. It removes the runtime dependency on permission and model choice.

2. The duplicate is acceptable as an unavoidable 21.8 KB token cost, but it is not always a no-op. Explicit invocation intentionally enables both modes, even if the user had stopped one.

3. Yes. Shrinking or reordering only moves the loss and will not fix the resumed-session case. Always-on reinjection covers both cases, and reinvocation is a workable fallback.

4. Yes. `au-unslop` is explicitly optional and upstream-exact, so implicit invocation preserves its stated standalone behavior. The README warning and clean-install test boundary make the interaction clear.

## Blocking

1. Lines 341 to 345: “its cache copy holds the skill, both reference files, and the hooks.” Problem: Revision 5 removes `references/`, so P9 contradicts P1 and would specify a failing load check. Exact change: replace this with “its cache copy byte-matches every shipped file, including the skill with both embedded upstream blocks and the hooks,” and say Codex checks the same.

2. Lines 95 to 98: “That costs tokens and changes nothing.” Problem: this conflicts with the lifecycle rule that explicit invocation enables both modes. A user who stopped unslop will notice it reactivate. Exact change: say the duplicate adds no new rules, but explicit invocation re-enables both modes; passive chunk reloads preserve known state.
